CUM-LABOUR COURT, PANDU NAGAR, KANPUR 

Industrial Dispute No. 11/1988 
In the matter of dispute between : 

The State Assistant General Secretary, U.P, Bank Emp¬ 
loyees Association, 36/1 Kailash Mandir, Kanpur. 

AND 

The Regional Manager, Bank of India, 15/54-B Viren- 
dra Smriti Complex, Civil Lines, Kanpur. 

AWARD 

1. The Central Government, Ministry of Labour, vide its 
notification no. L-12012/137/87-D-IKA) dated 3-2-88 has 
l el erred tho following dispute for adjudication to this Tri¬ 
bunal;— 

Whether the action of the management of Regional 
Manager, Bank of India, Kanpur in terminating 
the services of Sri Shiv Narain and not considering 
him for fuither employment while lecruiting freshi 
hands under See. 25H. of tho I.D. Act, was justi¬ 
fied? If not, to what relief is the workman en¬ 
titled? 

2 The industrial dispute on behalf of the woikman Sri 
Shiv Narain has been raised by the State Assistant General 
Seuctary, U.P. Bank Employees Union (hereinaftci referred 
to as Union) Kanpur. 

3. Ine case of the Union on behalf oi the wo.kman is 
Hint me tarns with a view to avoid the appunnment or 
pcinianent Hands adopted a practice to appoint temporary/ 
uany rated employees ror uoing the duties of a legu.ar 
H.iiiAic and to ucpnve suui worktrs or the oeneiiis ot IVioai- 
ntit Bank Awrnu. J fie Union runner p.eads dial such icm- 
poiaiy employees were teimmaieu/ 1 enciicnea aud liesn 
nanus appointed without giving enauce oi employment and/ 
or continuation ot se; vices to such tcmpouiy/teiminaiud 
employees, tins practice was a uevree to vionue me provi¬ 
sos ot me modiiied Bank Award and l.D. Act besides an 
au or Uutau' Labour Piucuco on tlie pait ot the manage¬ 
ment. Jt is tuitfiei averred by the Union that the woikman 
on Shiv Naram was appointed oy tnc Bank at its Hussan- 
gaaj Unnao branch as a Peon (Sepoy) on 20-8-8J and had 
worked over 105 days upto 4-2-8u excluding Sundays anc 
Holidays whereafter his services were tetnunated by the 
bunk without any reason or justification The Union allege: 
that the workman concerned was doing the duties of £ 
rcgular/permanent natuie of job of the subordinate staff bin 
was treated as a temporary ,hand The bank had ncithei 
issued any letter of appomtment/lettcr of termination it 
the name of workman Sri Shiv Narain nor ever paid com 
pensatlon. The woi kman was also not given any notice oi 
notice pay. The bank management has thus violated the

provisions of secs 25G a U *. il read with See, 25J of the 
ID Act and ID. (Centu 1 ) Rules including Rules 77 and 
78 of the stud Rules Voilation ot paras 493, 495, 507, 
516, 519, 522 and 524 ol the Snstry Awaid read with para 

20 7 and 20 8 of the Isl Bipartite Settlement have been 
pleaded by the Union besides voilation of Articles 14 16 and 

21 of the Constitution of India 

4 the Union has, thercfoie, piu\ed that the workman 
is entitled to be reinstated m setvicc with lull back wages 
and all consequential benefits 

5 The case of the Union is contested by tne management 
bank The bank deny the fact that it adopted practice to 
appoint temporary/daily rated employees in the manner 
alleged by the Union The bank plead that the casual 
persons ‘are employed whcncvci uoik of casual nature 
arises. It is wrong that the employment of such casual 
daily rated employees is violative of provisions of any award 
or Industrial Disputes Act or is Unfair I-abour Practice, 
The bank further pleads that Sri Shiv Naiain was engaged 
by t.he bank intermittently for short intervals. He never 
worked for more than 2 or 4 days continuously. He was 
engaged for 18 days m the year 1981, for 16 days in the 
year 1982, for 32 days in the year 1983, for 36 days in the 
year 1984 and for 3 days, in the year 1985 The manage 
ment has also gaves a month wise employment of the work¬ 
man in para 4 of its reply It is further avetred by the 
management that Sn Shiv Naiain a resident of same place 
where the bank’s Hassanganj Branch is situate in the rural 
area in the District Unnao Sn Shiv Naraln was engaged 
by the bank in case some staff was not available. It is 
denied by the bank that the sei vices of Sri Shiv Narain was 
terminated by It. 

6 . The bank fui thei deny that Su Shiv Narain was work¬ 
ing/doing the woik of a regular and peimauent nature. The 
bank also deny violation of any of the pio visions of Bi¬ 
partite Settlcmcnt/Sastry Awurd or Articles of the Consti¬ 
tution of India as has been alleged by tne Union in its 
claim statement. The head office of the bank by circular 
letter No PERSMLP.80/9 dated 31-12-80 laid down guide 
lines for making casual employment m subordinate cadre, 
particularly in case of Badli Sepoys. The bank decided to 
prepare a panel for casual employment According to the 
guidelines a casual appointment could be made for not more 
than 4 days at a lime and not for more than 60 days in a 
ycai Since Sri Shiv Narain was a casual employee it was 
not necessary foi the bank to consider hint for future emp¬ 
loyment m view of the policy decision taken by the bank 

7 The Union filed its rejoinder but no new facts have 
been alleged by it in the rejoinder. 

8 Wheieas in support of its case the Union examined 
Sri Shiv Narain, the management have in support of their 
case, examined Shn Mohd Ashrat Siddiqui, an officer of 
the bank. 

9 From the above pleadings of the parties it is clear that 
Sim Shiv Narain was engaged by the bank mtermittantly 
Whereas the Union has set up the case that the workman 
Sn Shiv Narain had in all worked for 105 days, the bank 
have admitted the above working of the workman. The 
management witness Sri Mohd. Ashrat Siddiqui has corro¬ 
borated this fact m para 3 of his affidavit Thus there re¬ 
mains no dispute between the parties that the workman had 
worked for 105 days during the penods 1981 to 1985 

10 The workman in para 3 of his cross examination has 
admitted that whenever a permanent member of sub-staff 
proceed on leave he was engaged by the bank. He has 
further deposed that he was never called by the bank for 
any interview in connection with the permanent appoint¬ 
ment by the bank. He fuither stated that his name was re¬ 
gistered with the Employment Exchange m the year 1979 
or 1980. He expressed his ignorance on the point that his 
name was ever sponsored by the Employment Exchange to 
the bank for mterview/test He was also unable to tell 
whether or not the bank conducted test/interview in the 
jear 1984 for the recruitment of sub staff In para 5 of 
his cross examination Sri Shiv Narain has further stated that 
one Sri Lalta Prasad worked in Bank’s Hnssanganj Branch 


and he was made permanent. He was unable to tell as to 
when Sn Lalta Prasad was made peimanent 

11 Fiom the above statement of the vvoikman it becomes 
abundantly clew that he was never subjected to regulai 
process of rcciuitmcnt in the subordinate cadie of the bank 
Being so he would be deemed to be a casual worker and 
not a temporary employee of the bank 

12 1 he management witness in paia 5 of his affidavit has 
deposed that Sri Shiv Naiain used to present himself at 
Hassanganj Branch at his will and whenever any permanent 
workman was absent, on leave or otherwise, the manager 
Hassanganj Branch used to employ Sii Shiv Naram or any 
othei person 'I he management witness was not conlronted 
by the authorised representative for the Union on this point 
This fact was also admitted by the workman in his cross 
examination 1 hus it stands proved that the engagement of 
Su Shiv Narain was against leave vacancy during the periods 
1981 to 1985, and in all he had worked for only 105 days 

13 Be it as it may, the question is whether the woikman 
is entitled to the benellts of the ID Act Admittedly the 
workman had worked for 10J days which is much less than 
240 days of work in a calender year, therefore, in his case 
the provisions of sec. 25F, 25G and 25H of the ID. Act 
would not apply. I-oi the application of section 25F ID 
Act, it is necessary that the workman must have been in 
continuous service foi not less than one year undei any 
employer prior to the date of his termination/retrenchment 
Section 25G of the ID Act is to be read with Rules 77 
of the I D. (Central) Rules and Sec 25H of the I D. Act is 
to be read with Rule 78 Rules 77 and 78 apply to a woik¬ 
man as defined in Rule 76 of the ID. (Central) Rules and 
according to Rule 76 such a workman should be a person 
who had worked continuously for not less than one year 
prim to the lamination of his services. Continuous one 
yeai service within the meaning of Section 25 ol ID Act, 
means working for 240 days atlcast during a year There¬ 
fore, nn his case the provisions of Section 25G, 2JH of the 
Act too are not applicable 

14 I may make it clear that it is a matter of common 
knowledge that in the banking industry there is a tegular 
procedure for the permanent recruitment in the bank’s sub¬ 
ordinate cadre Therefore, individual manage]s arte not 
empowered to recruit persons of their on accord. In view 
of it Sn Shiv Naiain can neved have any vested or legal right 
of claiming any appointment in the subordinate cadre of 
the bank or even claiming continuous temporary engage¬ 
ments ’against leave vacancies 

15 The authorised representative for the Union has argued 
that the management indulged in unfair labour practice by 
appointing temporary employees on casual basis. I find no 
foice in it The Union has not named persons either in its 
pleadings or in its rejoinder as to who were the persons who 
weic so appointed by the management prior to their appoint¬ 
ment. 

16 Thcrefoie, from the above discussions of facts and 
law I find that the Union has failed to prove his case that 
the workman was appointed against a leglar vacancy of 
a peon (Sepoy) by the management and also that the bank 
violated the provisions of modified Awards of the batik be¬ 
sides violations of Articles of the Constitution of India 

17 Hence il is held that the action of the management 
m terminating the services of the workman and not consider¬ 
ing him for further employment while recruiting fresh hands 
is justified Accordingly the Umon/workmnn is held en¬ 
titled to no relief 

»' Reference is answered accordingly. 

ARIAN DEV, Presiding Officer

New Delhi, the 14tli July. 1992 

S.O. 1992.—Whereas the Central Government having 
been satisfied that the public interest so required had in pur¬ 
suance of the provision of sub-clause (vi) of clause (n) of 
soction 2 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), 
declared by the notification of the Government of India in 
the Ministry of Labour S.O. No. 390 dated the 13th January, 
1992 the Indutsry for the supply of milk under the Delhi 
Milk Scheme to be a public utility service for the purposes 
of the said Act, for a period of six months from the 29th 
January, 1992; 

And, whereas, the Central Government is of opinion that 
public interest requires the extension of the said period by a 
further period of six months; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by the 
proviso to sub-clause (vi) of clause (n) of section 2 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby declares the said industriay to he a 
public Utility service for the purpose of the said Act, for a 
further period of six months from the 29th July, 1992.

New Delhi, the 14th July, 1992 

S.O. 1993,—Whereas the Central Government is satisfied 
the public interest requires that the Currency Note Press, 
which is covered by entry 25 in the first Schedule to the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), should he 
declared as a public utility service for the purposes of the 
said Act; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-clause (vl) of clause (n) of section 2 of the Industrial 
Disputes Art, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby declares with immediate effect the Currecy Note 
Press, Nashik Road to he a public utility service for the 
purposes of the said Act for a period of six months.
